# Media check of `college_boosters_2026.xlsx`

Prepared 2026-10-28. For each of the 69 schools and 3 conferences the question was: who do the sportswriters covering the program call its biggest or most important booster(s)? The media answer was built first (ESPN, The Athletic, On3, then 247Sports / Front Office Sports / Sportico / Yahoo / local beat papers) and then compared with the Summary sheet's Booster #1-3. Verdict scale: **Match** (sheet top 3 substantially agrees with coverage), **Partial** (overlap but a top media name is missing or a sheet name is over-ranked), **Mismatch** (coverage points at different people). Confidence: **high** = 2+ outlets agree, **medium** = one outlet, **low** = no clear media consensus. The Athletic is paywalled; findings based on headline and search snippet only are marked "snippet only".

## 2. Per-school detail

Flagged schools first, then the rest in the Summary sheet's order.

### Ohio State

**Spreadsheet top 3:** Brian Schottenstein / Cardale Jones / C.J. Stroud  
**Review flag:** Cardale Jones / C.J. Stroud are former players, not major funders; legacy donors (Wexner, Covelli, Crane) may better reflect power.  
**Verdict:** Partial — **Confidence:** medium

**Media consensus top boosters**

1. **Brian Schottenstein** — status 2025-26: Alive and active. Co-founder/'owner' of THE Foundation collective (est. 2022); still the collective's public face in Jan 2025 (ABC6). In 2025 Ohio State folded THE Foundation and The 1870 Society into the in-house Buckeye Sports Group and invited the collectives' founders/board members to serve as advisers (FOS, 2025-09-06), so his role is now advisory rather than operating a collective.
   - The Columbus Dispatch (via Yahoo Sports), "Second NIL collective supporting Ohio State football launches" (2022-04-20): THE Foundation is 'a group co-founded by booster and real estate developer Brian Schottenstein and former Buckeyes quarterback Cardale Jones.' — https://sports.yahoo.com/second-nil-collective-supporting-ohio-123143392.html
   - The Columbus Dispatch (Joey Kaufman, via Yahoo Sports), "How much money did Ohio State nonprofit NIL collectives raise after their launch?" (2024-02-13): Schottenstein, 'a Columbus real estate executive,' established THE Foundation in March 2022 with Cardale Jones; $2.4M raised in 2022, 'more than $4 million in donations last year'; board members matched $500,000 (board not named). Mark Stetson and the 1870 Society founders are not named. — https://sports.yahoo.com/much-money-did-ohio-state-181052799.html
   - ABC6 / WSYX Columbus (Kellyanne Stitts), "How THE Foundation is working to support roster retention" (2025-01-24): Schottenstein is the only Foundation figure named and quoted, called 'THE Foundation owner' (caption: co-founder); no individual donors named. — https://abc6onyourside.com/sports/the-football-fever/how-the-foundation-is-working-to-support-roster-retention
   - Buckeye Sports Bulletin, "BSB Interview Issue: Brian Schottenstein Enters The NIL Game" (2022-07-30): 'an Ohio State booster and president of the Schottenstein Real Estate Group' who founded THE Foundation with Cardale Jones; names Urban Meyer, D'Angelo Russell, Terry McLaurin and J.T. Barrett as board members. — https://buckeyesports.com/bsb-interview-issue-brian-schottenstein-enters-the-nil-game/
   - Front Office Sports (Amanda Christovich), "How Ohio State Rebuilt Its NIL Strategy in the Rev-Share Era" (2025-09-06): Buckeye Sports Group consolidated THE Foundation and The 1870 Society, took over their NIL contracts and donations, and invited the collectives' 'founders and board members' to serve as advisers; no donor or founder is named in the piece. — https://frontofficesports.com/how-ohio-state-rebuilt-its-nil-strategy-in-the-rev-share-era/
   - On3, "Ohio State NIL collective The Foundation leveraging Florida connections, plans for VIP club" (2023) *(snippet only)*: Headline/snippet: THE Foundation held a Miami donor event with Brian Hartline, Jeremiah Smith and Carnell Tate; plans a Florida chapter and VIP club. — https://www.on3.com/nil/news/ohio-state-nil-collective-the-foundation-leveraging-florida-connections-plans-for-vip-club
2. **Les Wexner** — status 2025-26: Alive (b. 1937) but embattled: 2025-26 coverage is entirely about Epstein-file revelations and demands to strip his name from campus buildings; Ohio State has so far refused to remove it from the Les Wexner Football Complex (WOSU 2026-01-28, 2026-10-08). Writers treat him as the historic 'deepest-pocketed' donor, not as an active athletics/NIL funder.
   - Mother Jones (Sam Brodey), "Meet the Big-Money Boosters Behind College Football's Top 25 Teams" (2014-09-05): Ohio State's booster is Les Wexner: 'The bra baron is worth $6 billion, making him the richest man in Ohio, and Ohio State's deepest-pocketed booster.' — https://adops.motherjones.com/?p=259546
   - Sportico, "Billionaire Boosters of College Football (2024 list)" (2024) *(snippet only)*: Search snippet: lists Wexner (net worth $7.9B) as Ohio State's billionaire booster; $100M 2011 gift, the largest in school history; football complex named for him; notes his Epstein friendship 'has colored the honor for some in the Columbus community.' Page fetch blocked (tollbit). — https://www.sportico.com/personalities/people/2024/billionaire-boosters-college-football-1234821228
   - Sports Illustrated (Ohio State on SI), "Ohio State Mulling Building Name Changes Due to Wexner's Alleged Jeffrey Epstein Ties" (2026-02-21): 'major OSU donor for years' who 'donated vast riches to his alma mater'; the Les Wexner Football Complex is among facilities named for him. — https://www.si.com/college/ohiostate/news/ohio-state-mulling-building-name-changes-due-to-wexner-s-alleged-jeffrey-epstein-ties-01khz3008vfr
   - WOSU 89.7 (via WYSO), "Ohio State denies request to remove Les Wexner's name from Woody Hayes Athletic Center" (2026-01-28): 'Wexner's donations amounted to $5 million' tied to naming the football complex; university denied a Strauss survivor's removal request. — https://www.wyso.org/2026-01-28/ohio-state-denies-request-to-remove-les-wexners-name-from-woody-hayes-athletic-center
   - WOSU 89.7, "Epstein, Strauss survivors call for Wexner's name to be removed from Ohio State buildings" (2026-10-08): Describes him as 'the Ohio billionaire'; his name remains on the 'Les Wexner Football Complex at the Woody Hayes Athletics Center.' — https://www.wosu.org/higher-education-and-k-12-education/2026-10-08/epstein-strauss-survivors-call-for-wexners-name-to-be-removed-from-ohio-state-buildings
3. **Mark Stetson (with Jason Priestas and Aidin Aghamiri), The 1870 Society backers** — status 2025-26: Active as of 2023-24 coverage; The 1870 Society was Ohio State football's 'premier' collective from 2023 until it was consolidated into Buckeye Sports Group in 2025 (FOS 2025-09-06). The 1870 Society's own site says standalone memberships ended in 2025. No 2025-26 outlet names Stetson individually.
   - On3, "Ohio State football taps The 1870 Society as premier NIL collective" (2023) *(snippet only)*: Snippet: AD Gene Smith named The 1870 Society the premier NIL collective for Ohio State football; it 'holds backing from a number of boosters, including Jason Priestas, Mark Stetson and Aidin Aghamiri.' Body returned 403. — https://www.on3.com/nil/news/ohio-state-buckeyes-football-the-1870-society-premier-nil-collective-gene-smith
   - On3 (collective profile), "The 1870 Society" (2023-2024) *(snippet only)*: Snippet: lists Mark Stetson as a founder, Todd Markiewicz as president, and 'backing from a number of boosters, including Jason Priestas, Mark Stetson and Aidin Aghamiri.' Body returned 403. — https://www.on3.com/nil/collectives/the-1870-society-267
   - The Athletic (cited by sheet), "Ohio State football NIL / transfer portal (2024-05-03)" (2024-05-03) *(snippet only)*: Sheet cites this article for Stetson as 1870 Society founder; nytimes.com could not be fetched, so not independently verified. — https://www.nytimes.com/athletic/5466694/2024/05/03/ohio-state-football-nil-transfer-portal/
   - Front Office Sports, "Ohio State Spending Marks Turning Point in CFB's Emerging Free Agency" (2024-01-24): Quotes 1870 Society VP Drew Esler ('everybody's put their money where their mouth is'); does not name Stetson. — https://frontofficesports.com/ohio-state-spending-marks-turning-point-in-cfbs-emerging-free-agency
4. **C.J. Stroud** — status 2025-26: Active NFL quarterback (Houston Texans). One documented gift to THE Foundation in January 2024 at the 'Platinum' $50,000-$100,000 tier; no later gift reported.
   - Front Office Sports, "Ohio State Spending Marks Turning Point in CFB's Emerging Free Agency" (2024-01-24): Stroud 'has become a major donor' to THE Foundation. — https://frontofficesports.com/ohio-state-spending-marks-turning-point-in-cfbs-emerging-free-agency
   - CBS Sports, "C.J. Stroud gives back to Ohio State as Texans star QB makes significant donation to Buckeyes' NIL collective" (2024-01) *(snippet only)*: Snippet: Stroud joined THE Foundation's Founders Club as a Platinum Donor; the website puts that tier at $50,000-$100,000. — https://www.cbssports.com/college-football/news/c-j-stroud-gives-back-to-ohio-state-as-texans-star-qb-makes-significant-donation-to-buckeyes-nil-collective
   - On3, "CJ Stroud makes 'platinum' contribution to Ohio State's NIL collective" (2024-01) *(snippet only)*: Snippet: 'platinum' contribution to THE Foundation (headline only; body 403). — https://www.on3.com/college/ohio-state-buckeyes/news/cj-stroud-makes-major-contribution-to-ohio-states-nil-collective
   - Front Office Sports, "CJ Stroud Writes a Check to Ohio State's NIL Collective Amid a Big Offseason for the Buckeyes" (2024-01) *(snippet only)*: Snippet: gift of between $50,000 and $100,000, the collective's second-highest tier. — https://frontofficesports.com/cj-stroud-writes-a-check-to-ohio-states-nil-collective-amid-a-big-offseason-for-the-buckeyes

**Comparison with spreadsheet**

| Name | Label | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Brian Schottenstein | MATCH | Dispatch (2022, 2024), Buckeye Sports Bulletin (2022) and ABC6 (Jan 2025) all identify him as the booster/owner behind THE Foundation; the only individual writers consistently attach to Buckeye NIL money. |
| Cardale Jones | OVER-RANKED | Every outlet (Dispatch, CBS, BSB, NBC4) describes him as 'former Buckeyes quarterback' and co-founder/GM of THE Foundation; no outlet calls him a funder or top booster. Operator, not money. |
| C.J. Stroud | OVER-RANKED | FOS (Jan 2024) did call him a 'major donor', and CBS/On3 covered the gift, but it is a single $50k-$100k-tier contribution; no outlet ranks him among the program's top boosters and nothing since Jan 2024. |
| Les Wexner | LEGACY | Sheet files him under 'Also notable'; Mother Jones (2014) and Sportico (2024) both name him as Ohio State's deepest-pocketed/billionaire booster, and he is the only Ohio State donor national writers single out. 2025-26 coverage is the Epstein naming controversy, not current giving, so 'legacy' is defensible, but if the sheet wants a #2 the writers' answer is Wexner, not Jones or Stroud. |
| Mark Stetson (1870 Society) | LEGACY | On3 (2023) names him with Jason Priestas and Aidin Aghamiri as the boosters backing The 1870 Society, Ohio State's 'premier' football collective 2023-25. Sheet has him under 'Also notable'; writers treated the 1870 backers as current money through 2024, but the collective was absorbed into Buckeye Sports Group in 2025. |

The sheet's The Athletic citation (2026-08-12, 'third-highest Razorback Foundation donor') could not be opened or surfaced in search, so it is unverified. #2 is not supported by sportswriters. |

Writers rank him alongside Tyson: 'fellow booster' who co-funded the Calipari package (CBS/SI 2024) and 'one of the more prolific boosters in Arkansas athletics history' (SI 2025-11-05) - but 'rather quiet lately' and U.S. Ambassador to the UK since May 2025. Arguably the media #2 ahead of the Waltons. |
| J.B. and Johnelle Hunt family | MATCH | Sheet rank 5. SI (2025-11-05) names Johnelle Hunt only as the state's richest billionaire, and SI (2024-06-27) lists 'JB Hunt' among fans' imagined donors; no writer describes current athletics giving. Consistent with the sheet's 'historic' framing. |
| Jerry and Gene Jones | MATCH | Sheet rank 6. Fortune (2026-03) cites the 2015 $10.65M gift; SI (2024-04-21) and On3 (2024-04) discuss his reported Calipari-era NIL offer but say it is unclear how much he actually writes in checks. |

**Notes:** Tyson (#1) and Fletcher (#3) are confirmed by multiple sportswriters; the sheet's #2 Walton family is the weak spot - 2026 coverage says their athletics giving has slowed, and writers instead pair Tyson with Warren Stephens (sheet #4). Football-specific coverage in 2025-26 is about institutional money rather than individuals: the Razorback Foundation's ALL IN Fund raised nearly $25M toward a $50M goal (Talk Business 2026-08-06, https://talkbusiness.net/2026/08/razorback-foundation-football-fund-boosts-goal-to-50-million/), an unnamed donor gave seven figures after five minutes with Silverfield (Arkansas on SI 2025-12-05, https://www.si.com/college/arkansas/football/arkansas-now-in-top-half-of-sec-financially-to-start-silverfield-era), and SI (2025-11-05) reports a booster-class dispute with Yurachek in which Stephens's lieutenant Kevin Scanlon (Razorback Foundation chairman) is floated as an AD alternative. The Athletic article pages could not be opened (nytimes.com blocked) and no Athletic result surfaced in search.

**Flag assessment:** No review flag on the sheet. None needed for #1/#3; a flag is warranted on #2 (Walton family) - writers treat them as a slowed/legacy donor, and Warren Stephens is the better-supported #2 despite his ambassadorship.

The names writers do use are the same three the sheet lists, just in a different order (Jordan first on national lists; Shields and Hinton on facility-gift coverage). Confidence is medium because each name rests on one or two outlets, several of them local or syndicated (WNDU, EssentiallySports, FanSided) rather than ESPN/Athletic, and the Sportico body could not be opened. No ESPN or Athletic article naming an individual Notre Dame booster surfaced in searches.

**Flag assessment:** No review flag on the sheet and none warranted; the first-pass 'OVER-RANKED' labels on Shields and Hinton are withdrawn now that sportswriter coverage of both has been found.

## 3. Schools where the spreadsheet most needs fixing

### 3a. Priority fixes (curated)

These are the rows where the evidence is strongest that the top 3 is wrong, out of date, or contradicted by beat coverage. Mismatch verdicts come first.

- **Texas** (Mismatch, low confidence): None of the sheet's top 3 (Arnold/Itkin, Folger, Durant) has any sportswriter footprint; writers name car-dealer Christopher Late (ESPN, Aug 2025) and TRT's Robert Rowling (single outlet). Durant's last gift is 2018.
- **Washington** (Mismatch, low confidence): McFarlan, Gittinger and Crockett are Tyee-roster names with no media coverage; beat writers say UW has no identifiable mega-donor and Montlake Futures closed in 2025. Flag confirmed.
- **Purdue** (Mismatch, low confidence): Allen, Stoker and Martin appear only in Purdue releases; writers name Drew Brees (Boilermaker Alliance), the Rohrman family and Dick Buell.
- **Mississippi (Ole Miss)** (Mismatch, medium confidence): The $5M facility donors at #1-3 are olemiss.edu-only; the one booster writers call 'prominent' (CBS, Jan 2026) is Matt Bowers, at sheet rank 5. Walker Jones belongs in the current tier.
- **South Carolina** (Mismatch, low confidence): Top 3 is a corporate field-logo deal plus two anonymous gifts; the named boosters writers cite (Joe Rice, Dodie Anderson) are both deceased, Bodiford is missing.
- **Minnesota** (Mismatch, low confidence): T. Denny Sanford died 2026-07-18 and must leave the current tier; Lindahl/Pearson have no 2023-26 coverage; the only NIL-era donor writers name is Bob Klas. Flag confirmed.
- **Duke** (Partial, high confidence): Writers (WSJ, Apr 2025, via Bleacher Report) have named the One Vision Futures Fund founders Jeff Fox, Dan Levitan and Steve Duncker; Heath Freeman at #1 rests on a goduke.com release. Flag partly overtaken.
- **Ohio State** (Partial, medium confidence): Cardale Jones and C.J. Stroud are ex-players, not major funders (Stroud: one $50-100k gift); Les Wexner is the only legacy name writers call the biggest booster. Flag confirmed.
- **Tennessee** (Partial, medium confidence): Kamara and Grant Williams have no booster coverage; the Haslams are the consensus, and writers treat Spyre's Baddour/Clawson as current power brokers. Flag confirmed.
- **Alabama** (Partial, low confidence): Bryant Jr., Justin Thomas and the Sabans are never called boosters by any writer; coverage is about unnamed boosters agitating over DeBoer. Headline ('no single mega-booster') is right, the names are not. Flag confirmed.

## 4. Method notes

- Search order per school: ESPN, The Athletic, On3, then 247Sports, Front Office Sports, Sportico, Yahoo Sports and the local beat outlet. Paywalled Athletic pieces that could not be opened are marked "snippet only".
- A name was counted only when a writer explicitly called the person a top/major booster, power broker, key donor, collective bankroller, or the person behind a coaching hire or firing. Passing mentions, sponsors, administrators and coaches were not counted unless the writer said they personally give major money.
- Where coverage names nobody, the detail section says "no clear media consensus" and the verdict is judged against that: a sheet built from university gift announcements can be reasonable even where beat writers do not single out one booster.
- No dollar figures or quotes were invented; each line cites the URL it came from. Content-farm URLs were ignored.
